Once formed, PQPs cool and evolve intro mini neutron stars or stable strange matter nuggets, depending on the QCD ground state. Formation probabilities of $10^{-9}$ to $10^{-4}$ per horizon volume could suffice to reproduce the present dark matter density without altering Big Bang Nucleosynthesis or requiring entropy dilution. PQPs completely evade microlensing constraints if their mass is below $10^{-7}$ solar masses, a range that could easily accommodate most of the dark matter. PQPs thus potentially constitute a conservative, Standard-Model based, and observationally testable solution to the dark matter puzzle.

</details>


### [156] [Migdal ionization as a probe of light dark matter from Nuclear Transition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10716)
*Yuanchao Lou,Liangliang Su,Lei Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel strategy for detecting light dark matter generated by nuclear reactors, utilizing the Migdal effect in germanium detectors, and establishes new limits on dark matter interactions based on experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct detection of reactor-produced light dark matter is challenging due to conventional methods being hindered by quenching effects, necessitating innovative detection strategies.

Method: The authors utilized the Migdal effect in germanium detectors, combined with ON-OFF residual spectra analysis from the TEXONO experiment, to search for signals of light dark matter.

Result: New stringent limits on the interaction between dark matter and nuclei were established, covering a mass range of $0.01\,	ext{MeV}	ext{ to }2.6\,	ext{MeV}$.

Conclusion: The findings provide complementary constraints to existing astrophysical and cosmological bounds on light dark matter.

Abstract: Nuclear reactors serve as a key artificial source of light dark matter. Direct detection of reactor-produced dark matter faces substantial obstacles, since quenching effects suppress conventional elastic scattering signals below detector thresholds. We present a new search strategy utilizing the Migdal effect in germanium detectors to probe light dark matter produced via nuclear de-excitation from reactors. Using ON-OFF residual spectra from the TEXONO experiment, we set a new stringent limit on the dark matter and nucleus interaction over the mass range $0.01\,\text{MeV}\le m_χ\lesssim 2.6\,\text{MeV}$, which provides a complementary bound to existing cosmological and astrophysical limits.

</details>


### [157] [The KM3NeT 220 PeV event: a neutrino messenger from the grand unification scale?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10760)
*Vernon Barger*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes the 220 PeV neutrino KM3-230213A, proposing it as evidence for a relic particle decaying into neutrinos, establishing links to dark matter and grand-unified theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the implications of the 220 PeV neutrino observation and its compatibility with established limits from IceCube and Auger.

Method: The study presents a theoretical framework for the decay of a relic particle into neutrinos, calculating relevant parameters such as lifetime and coupling constants.

Result: The proposed model correlates the neutrino flux with the particle's lifetime and effective coupling, suggesting a grand-unified scale near 10^16 GeV.

Conclusion: The findings support the existence of a relic particle related to dark matter and provide a method to differentiate this scenario from alternative astrophysical explanations.

Abstract: The 220~PeV neutrino KM3-230213A, in tension with IceCube and Auger limits unless it is a narrow transient feature, admits a two-body reading: the decay $X \to ν\barν$ of a relic with $m_X = 4.4\times10^{8}$~GeV. The flux fixes $τ/f$, lifetime over dark-matter fraction, and the lifetime fixes an effective coupling $G_{\rm eff} \sim 10^{-46}$~GeV$^{-2}$; for a flavored onium decaying through one electroweak-Higgs insertion the mediator lands near $10^{16}$~GeV, so energy and rate together read out the grand-unified scale. We develop the construction and its tests against the astrophysical alternative.

</details>


### [158] [A Unified Study of Hidden-Charm and Hidden-Bottom Mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10829)
*Vikas Patel,Chetan Lodha,A. K. Rai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper offers a comprehensive analysis of hidden-charm and hidden-bottom mesons, utilizing a unified framework to derive mass spectra, decay constants, and transition form factors, while exploring their interrelations and spectra characteristics in both sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a coherent understanding of hidden-charm and hidden-bottom mesons within a unified theoretical framework.

Method: Utilization of a screened Coulomb-plus-confining interaction and QCD sum rules to analyze mass spectra, decay constants, and radiative transitions.

Result: The analysis reveals mass spectra, state assignments, and transition characteristics consistent with expected properties of charmonium and bottomonium, highlighting differences in sensitivity to various phenomena.

Conclusion: This work establishes a solid benchmark for future studies in meson spectroscopy and related QCD observables.

Abstract: We present a unified phenomenological analysis of hidden-charm and hidden-bottom mesons, treating the \(c\bar c\) and \(b\bar b\) sectors within a common screened-potential and QCD sum-rule framework. The mass spectra are obtained from a screened Coulomb-plus-confining interaction with spin-dependent corrections, while the short-distance decay constants, annihilation widths and electromagnetic transition form factors are organized through two-point and three-point QCD sum rules. The same calculated states are then used to study E1 and M1 radiative transitions, Regge trajectories and finite-spectrum thermodynamic observables constructed from excitation energies relative to the corresponding ground states. The results are discussed together rather than as disconnected outputs: the mass spectra determine the state assignments, the Regge plots test the global level ordering, the radiative widths probe orbital and spin-flip overlap integrals, and the QCD sum-rule quantities connect the spectrum to current-coupled observables. The comparison of the two hidden-flavour sectors shows the expected compression and stronger spin suppression in bottomonium, while charmonium remains more sensitive to fine-structure, radial-node and medium-related effects. The resulting picture provides a coherent vacuum benchmark for \(c\bar c\) and \(b\bar b\) spectroscopy, radiative transitions and QCD sum-rule observables.

</details>


### [159] [Multipole structure of the $N \to Δ$ Transition Generalized Parton Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10901)
*June-Young Kim,Hyun-Chul Kim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper investigates the multipole structure of the $N 	o Δ$ transition using generalized parton distributions, decomposing transition GPDs into monopole, dipole, and quadrupole components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance understanding of the $N 	o Δ$ transition dynamics through the analysis of multipole components and their correspondence with helicity amplitudes.

Method: The study employs a multipole expansion of the covariant transition matrix element, analyzing the transition GPDs and establishing a relationship with light-front helicity amplitudes.

Result: Identified one monopole, two dipole, and one quadrupole component, with implications for impact-parameter transition densities.

Conclusion: The multipole transition densities provide a visualization of partonic structures, improving comprehension of the $N 	o Δ$ transition in the transverse plane.

Abstract: We establish the multipole structure of the $N \to Δ$ transition at the level of the generalized parton distributions (GPDs). We decompose the four transition GPDs into one monopole, two dipole, and one quadrupole components in the transverse plane by a multipole expansion of the covariant transition matrix element in terms of the three-dimensional spin-transition tensors and the transverse momentum transfer. These multipole components are in one-to-one correspondence with the light-front helicity amplitudes. In the zero-skewness limit, the multipole GPDs define impact-parameter transition densities, which generalize the transverse transition charge densities to the $x$-dependent level. These transition densities arise from non-diagonal matrix elements between two distinct hadronic states and must therefore be distinguished from the diagonal densities of the nucleon and the $Δ$. The multipole transition densities visualize the monopole, dipole, and quadrupole structures of the partonic $N \to Δ$ transition in the transverse plane.

</details>


### [160] [The Impossible Triangle: A No-Go for Symmetry-Protected Scalar Portals in Interacting Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10859)
*Mohid Farhan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines the challenges of embedding interacting dark energy (IDE) within four portals in a $Z_2$-symmetric model due to severe fine-tuning required to address the $S_8$ tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses the $S_8$ tension, which suggests the need for interacting dark energy and highlights challenges when trying to embed this in natural, UV-complete physics.

Method: The authors analyze four symmetry-protected DM--DE portals: quartic, trilinear, derivative, and Yukawa within a $Z_2$-symmetric Inert Doublet + Singlet Model.

Result: The trilinear portal requires an exceptionally large coupling constant ($g 	ext{ of } 10^{-16}	ext{ GeV}$) leading to extreme tuning ($Δ 	ext{ of } 10^{52}$). The quartic portal also demands unrealistic coupling values, while the derivative portal has limited dynamic suppression, and the Yukawa portal maintains a high tuning requirement.

Conclusion: No single mediator model meets the criteria of technical naturalness and resolves the $S_8$ tension; viable solutions involve tuning or symmetry breaking.

Abstract: The $S_8$ tension motivates interacting dark energy (IDE), but embedding IDE in UV-complete physics faces severe naturalness challenges. We analyze four symmetry-protected DM--DE portals ( quartic ($\tfrac{1}{2}λφ^2χ^2$), trilinear ($gφχ^2$), derivative ($(c_6/Λ^2)(\partial_μφ)^2χ^2$), and fermionic Yukawa ($yφ\barψψ$) )within a $Z_2$-symmetric Inert Doublet + Singlet Model. The trilinear portal requires $β\sim 0.45$ ($g \sim 10^{-16}\,\mathrm{GeV}$), overshooting the radiative bound $g \lesssim 10^{-42}\,\mathrm{GeV}$ by $\sim 26$ orders (tuning $Δ\sim 10^{52}$). The quartic portal needs $λ\sim \mathcal{O}(1\text{--}10)$ versus $λ\lesssim 10^{-86}$ ($Δ\sim 10^{87}$). The derivative portal saturates dynamically at $\lesssim 4\%$ suppression. The Yukawa portal yields $Δ\sim 10^{52}$, persisting even with SUSY cancellation. No single-mediator model simultaneously satisfies technical naturalness and resolves the $S_8$ tension. Viable solutions require either multi-field tuned cancellations or explicit symmetry breaking with quantified fine-tuning.

</details>


### [161] [Determination of the Odderon amplitude in elastic cross-sections at high energies from scaling and analyticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11286)
*J. A. velazquez Corral,B. G. Giraud,R. Peschanski,C. Royon*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines the scaling amplitudes in $pp$ and $p\bar p$ elastic scattering, confirming the scaling principles at the LHC and Tevatron, deriving the Pomeron amplitude, and addressing discrepancies in data associated with the Odderon amplitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish consistency in the scaling amplitudes of elastic scattering at high energies and resolve discrepancies observed in experimental data, especially the dip observed in the $pp$ differential cross-sections.

Method: The authors apply a general energy-to-phase conversion based on S-Matrix principles, analyze TOTEM $pp$ differential cross-sections, and determine the Odderon contribution in the dip/bump region using fitting techniques.

Result: The study finds tension with experimental data at moderate momentum transfer for $pp$ scattering and identifies the negative signature Odderon contribution, allowing a parameter-free prediction for $p\bar p$ scattering that aligns with D0 measurements.

Conclusion: The extraction and discussion of the Odderon amplitude provide meaningful insights into the complex interactions during elastic scattering and contribute to a better understanding of particle collisions at high energies.

Abstract: Scaling amplitudes describing $pp$ elastic scattering differential cross-sections in the dip-bump region of momentum transfer at the LHC have been recently derived~\cite{scaling}. We check that the same scaling is verified by the $p\bar p$ cross-section at the highest energy of the Tevatron. Applying the general "energy to phase" relation for a given signature, coming from the analiticity properties inherent to the S-Matrix formalism~\cite{chew}, we derive the scaling amplitude with positive signature (i.e. the Pomeron). Fitting the $pp$ differential cross-sections measured by the TOTEM collaboration leads to some tension with data in the experimental dip observed at moderate momentum transfer. Concentrating the study to the dip/bump region, we are able to determine a contribution of a negative signature amplitude (i.e. the Odderon) leading to a parameter free prediction for the $p\bar p$ differential cross-section which is in agreement with the D0 data. The extraction of the Odderon amplitude in both modulus and phase is then performed and discussed.

</details>


### [162] [Conversion-Driven Baryogenesis in Flavored Dark Matter Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11147)
*Benedetta Belfatto,Monika Blanke,Jan Heisig,Lena Rathmann,Felix Wilsch*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores lepton-flavored dark matter and baryon asymmetry through conversion-driven freeze-out, detailing chemical-equilibrium conditions and extending the framework to quark-philic scenarios with Soft displaced decay products, emphasizing the importance of thermal effects and their implications for LHC searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the unresolved issues of dark matter and baryon asymmetry in fundamental physics, using a novel mechanism that connects these phenomena.

Method: The paper develops a mechanism based on conversion-driven freeze-out, analyzing chemical equilibrium conditions and extending the framework to scenarios with baryon-number-conserving CP-violating conversions of mediators into quarks and dark matter.

Result: The inclusion of strong QCD interactions, including bound-state formation, enlarges the viable parameter space, allowing for dark matter masses ranging from several hundred GeV to the TeV scale.

Conclusion: The study predicts long-lived particle signatures with soft displaced decay products, suggesting the need for dedicated searches at the HL-LHC.

Abstract: The dark matter and baryon asymmetry problems remain two of the most pressing questions in fundamental physics. Considering lepton-flavored dark matter, it has recently been shown that the cogenesis of dark matter and the baryon asymmetry can be economically achieved via conversion-driven freeze-out. This mechanism leverages semi-efficient conversions to drive a departure from equilibrium while preserving independence from initial conditions through early thermalization of the dark sector. In this work, we develop this mechanism further, providing a detailed analysis of the chemical-equilibrium conditions and demonstrating that the framework can be extended to quark-philic scenarios, where the matter-antimatter asymmetry is generated resonantly through baryon-number-conserving $CP$-violating conversions of a mediator field into Standard Model quarks and dark matter. The strong QCD interactions of the colored mediator, including bound-state formation effects during freeze-out, substantially enlarge the viable parameter space and allow dark matter masses from a few hundred GeV up to the TeV scale. We furthermore assess the impact of thermal effects by comparing a minimal treatment with a setup that approximately accounts for thermal masses and their kinematic consequences. The resulting scenario predicts striking long-lived particle signatures with soft displaced decay products that remain only partially explored at the LHC and motivate dedicated searches at the HL-LHC.

</details>


### [163] [Unitarization Schemes for High-Energy Elastic Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11504)
*E. G. S. Luna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper analyzes eikonal and $U$-matrix unitarization schemes in high-energy elastic scattering, focusing on Pomeron exchange and the effects of trajectory forms on parameter extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the high-energy behavior of elastic scattering and how it is influenced by Pomeron exchange.

Method: The authors apply eikonal and $U$-matrix unitarization schemes to assess elastic scattering amplitudes and compare linear versus nonlinear Pomeron trajectories.

Result: Findings indicate that the choice of trajectory form significantly affects the extracted Pomeron parameters and their stability in describing data.

Conclusion: Parameter dependence on trajectory form is crucial for phenomenological accuracy in high-energy scattering analyses.

Abstract: We examine key features of the eikonal and $U$-matrix unitarization schemes and apply both frameworks to investigate the high-energy behavior of the elastic scattering amplitude, considering input amplitudes whose asymptotic behavior is driven exclusively by Pomeron exchange. Particular attention is devoted to assessing how the extracted Pomeron parameters depend on the assumed trajectory form. In this context, we compare linear and nonlinear Pomeron trajectories, evaluating their impact on the phenomenological description of the data and on the stability of the fitted parameters.

</details>


### [164] [Soft photon approximation in a laser field: applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11160)
*Peter A. Krachkov,Simon V. Sorokin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents an application of the soft photon approximation in QED processes under a strong laser field, yielding new expressions for photon emissions and outperforming classical theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the understanding of photon emission in QED within intense laser fields, particularly through a simplified framework for multiple emissions.

Method: Utilizing the soft photon approximation for QED processes, new compact emission expressions were derived and applied to single, double, and N-photon Compton scattering.

Result: The soft photon approximation accurately reproduces the exact spectrum for single Compton scattering with an error of $	ext{O}(ω/ε)$ and provides analytical expressions for double Compton scattering across different energy regimes.

Conclusion: This work significantly enhances theoretical tools for analyzing multiple soft photon emissions in high-intensity laser fields.

Abstract: We apply the soft photon approximation to QED processes in a strong plane-wave laser field. New compact expressions for the emission factors are presented, which simplify the generalization to multiple soft photon emission. The method is applied to nonlinear single, double, and $N$-photon Compton scattering. For single Compton scattering, we show that the soft photon approximation reproduces the exact spectrum with accuracy $\mathcal{O}(ω/\varepsilon)$, significantly outperforming the classical radiation theory. For double Compton scattering, we derive analytical expressions for the differential probability in two kinematic regimes: when both photons are soft, and when one photon is soft while the other has arbitrary energy. We present a compact analytical expression for the amplitude of $N$-photon emission within the soft photon approximation. A numerical implementation in Wolfram Mathematica for single and double Compton scattering is provided.

</details>


### [165] [An improved moment QCD sum rule](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11536)
*Jin-Peng Zhang,Xu-Liang Chen,Wei Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents an improved moment sum rule (IMSR) framework in QCD sum rules, addressing major shortcomings of existing methods such as the Laplace sum rule and conventional moment sum rule, notably inconsistencies in ground-state mass extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve significant issues in existing QCD sum rule methods, particularly the subjective constraints of Laplace sum rules and the limitations of conventional moment sum rules in extracting coupling constants and consistent mass values.

Method: An improved moment sum rule framework explicitly incorporating quark-hadron duality and rigorous dependence conditions on unphysical parameters to control their influence in determining the duality parameter and ground-state mass.

Result: The IMSR method was applied to a pseudoscalar tetraquark system, yielding results that align well with previous Laplace sum rule analyses, demonstrating improved accuracy and reliability.

Conclusion: The IMSR framework enhances the robustness of QCD sum rules and eliminates subjectivity, providing a more reliable tool for hadron physics.

Abstract: QCD sum rules are among the most important non-perturbative tools in hadron physics, with the Laplace sum rule (LSR) and moment sum rule (MSR) being the two most commonly used formulations. Despite their widespread application, both approaches have significant shortcomings: the LSR relies on subjective criteria -- namely OPE convergence and pole dominance -- to constrain the parameter space, while the conventional MSR cannot extract the coupling constant of the interpolating current. More critically, the ground-state masses obtained from these two methods are often inconsistent. In this work, we propose an improved moment sum rule (IMSR) framework that resolves these issues simultaneously. Our method explicitly incorporates quark-hadron duality, which introduces the approximation condition on the OPE side and provides a natural a posteriori constraint on the parameters. We impose rigorous dependence conditions on the unphysical parameters $(Q^2_0,n)$ to quantify and control their influence. As a result, our framework uniquely determines both the optimal value of the duality parameter and the ground-state mass, without invoking any ad hoc or subjective criteria. It also allows for the simultaneous extraction of the current coupling. Applying the IMSR to a pseudoscalar $ud\Bar{d}\Bar{s}$ tetraquark system, the results are in excellent agreement with our previous LSR analyses, validating the effectiveness of the proposed scheme. The IMSR method substantially enhances the robustness and reliability of QCD sum rules, effectively eliminating the subjectivity that has long plagued conventional formulations.

</details>


### [166] [Neutron tagging of heavy lepton pair production at RHIC and LHC energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11224)
*Atacan Fatih Candar,Mehmet Cem Güçlü*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper calculates cross sections for electron-positron, muon-antimuon, and tau-antitau production in ultraperipheral heavy-ion collisions and compares these to experimental data from major collider experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance understanding of particle production mechanisms in ultraperipheral collisions and provide comparisons to experimental findings.

Method: The authors use Lowest Order Quantum Electrodynamics in conjunction with a Monte Carlo technique to compute impact parameter dependent probabilities, considering neutron emissions modeled with Poisson statistics and categorizing cross sections into relevant neutron classes.

Result: The computed cross sections are compared with measurements from STAR, ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS experiments across various center of mass energies, enabling insights into their accuracy and consistency with theoretical predictions.

Conclusion: The findings bridge theoretical calculations and experimental data, offering a detailed categorization of cross sections in relation to neutron emission scenarios.

Abstract: We calculated cross sections for $γγ\rightarrow \ell^+\ell^-$ ($\ell=e,μ,τ$) production in ultraperipheral gold and lead collisions and compare our results with recent measurements from the STAR, ALICE, ATLAS, and CMS experiments at center of mass energies $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=200$ GeV, $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=2.76 $ TeV, and $\sqrt{s_{NN}}=5.02$ TeV. The calculations are performed within lowest order quantum electrodynamics (LO QED) using a Monte Carlo technique for calculating the impact parameter dependent probability, and subsequently combined with neutron emission probabilities associated with Coulomb excitation of the nuclei. These neutron emission scenarios are modeled using a Poisson statistics, allowing the cross sections to be categorized into experimentally relevant neutron classes: 0n0n, 0n1n, 1n1n, 0nXn, XnXn, and inclusive (0n0n+0nXn+XnXn) production. To enable a direct comparison with experiments, fiducial phase space restrictions corresponding to the acceptance of each detector are implemented.

</details>


### [167] [Linearity, Nonlinearity and Universality of Regge Trajectories in Light Mesons: a Statistical Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11583)
*S. S. Afonin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the mass spectra of light non-strange mesons by comparing two linear Regge models against benchmark datasets, concluding that one model becomes statistically preferable after excluding certain states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the mass spectra of light non-strange mesons through Regge trajectories is crucial for advancing theoretical and phenomenological insights in particle physics.

Method: The authors employ a statistical analysis of two linear Regge models using data from the Particle Data Group and other sources, evaluating their performance through various criteria, including RSS, AIC, and BIC.

Result: Model 1 is favored over Model 2 in the full benchmark dataset, but after excluding $S$-wave states, the performance difference dissipates, and Model 2 is considered more parsimonious.

Conclusion: The findings suggest that mass spectra are better described by a nonlinear correction for $S$-wave states and that the second model effectively accounts for meson mass distribution in a broader sample.

Abstract: The masses of light non-strange mesons can be parameterized as a function of the radial quantum number $n$ and the orbital angular momentum $L$. We perform a comprehensive statistical and phenomenological comparative analysis of competing Regge-like formulas evaluated against two experimental datasets: a benchmark sample of 27 well-established states from the 2024 Particle Data Group (PDG) data, and an expanded sample of 85 states compiled within a recently proposed $(L,n)$-classification. Two linear Regge models for $M^2(L,n)$ are tested: one featuring distinct slopes for $L$ and $n$, $M^2(L,n)\propto an+bL$ (Model 1), and another assuming a universal slope, $M^2(L,n)\propto a(n+L)$ (Model 2). Model selection is conducted quantitatively using the Residual Sum of Squares (RSS), the adjusted RSS, the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC), and the Bayesian Information Criterion (BIC). Within the 27 benchmark states, Model 2 is statistically disfavored. We argue, however, that this discrepancy is driven by the specific behavior of $S$-wave ($L=0$) states. Upon their exclusion, the difference in performance between the two models becomes statistically insignificant, with information criteria selecting Model 2 as the more parsimonious description. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the masses of $S$-wave states can be successfully accommodated by a physically motivated, nonlinear $L$-dependent correction. A parallel analysis of the extended 85-state dataset, where the relative contribution of $S$-wave states is significantly smaller, reveals that Model 2 is statistically preferred, thereby restoring the Coulomb-like degeneracy in the Regge spectrum under consideration.

</details>


### [168] [Multi-scale improved predictions for $\boldsymbol{pp \to t\bar{t}W^+ +X}$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11652)
*Nikolaos Dimitrakopoulos,Malgorzata Worek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper compares NLO QCD predictions with MiNLO approach results for complex lepton decay processes at LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the accuracy of off-shell predictions in multi-lepton decay channels and improve scale settings.

Method: Comparison of standard NLO predictions and MiNLO results utilizing dynamic scale settings and Sudakov form factors in the Helac-NLO framework.

Result: Detailed comparison of integrated and differential cross-sections shows improvements in predictions using the MiNLO approach.

Conclusion: Merging various off-shell predictions enhances the description of the complex decay processes in proton-proton collisions.

Abstract: We compare standard fixed-order NLO QCD predictions for the full off-shell $pp \to e^+ν_e\, μ^-\barν_μ\, τ^+ν_τ\, b\bar{b} \, (j) + X$ processes in the multi-lepton decay channel with the results obtained using the MiNLO approach. The MiNLO method, now also implemented in the Helac-NLO framework, provides a dynamic determination of the renormalization and factorization scale settings by identifying the most likely branching histories of the additional jets through an inverse $k_T$-clustering algorithm. The inclusion of Sudakov form factors accounts for the large logarithms that arise in the presence of widely separated scales. We perform a dedicated comparison of the two approaches at both the integrated and differential (fiducial) cross-section levels for the LHC Run II energy of $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. Finally, we present the merging of the full off-shell predictions for $pp \to e^+ν_e\, μ^-\barν_μ\, τ^+ν_τ\, b\bar{b} \, + X$, $pp \to e^+ν_e\, μ^-\barν_μ\, τ^+ν_τ\, b\bar{b} \, j +X$ and $pp \to e^+ν_e\, μ^-\barν_μ\, τ^+ν_τ\, b\bar{b} \, jj$, with the aim of improving the description of the underlying $pp \to e^+ν_e\, μ^-\barν_μ\, τ^+ν_τ\, b\bar{b} + X$ process.

</details>


### [169] [Coulomb Effects in Momentum-Space Femtoscopy: A Case Study of the $\bar{K}Ω$ System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11321)
*Pablo Encarnación,Amador García-Lorenzo,Miguel Albaladejo,Albert Feijoo,Juan Nieves,Isaac Vidaña*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a momentum-space framework for modeling Coulomb interactions in femtoscopic correlation functions, utilizing a modified method conducive to numerical application, and explores pseudoscalar-baryon interactions as well as their implications in hadron interactions analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a reliable and practical method for incorporating Coulomb effects in femtoscopic correlation analyses, facilitating better extraction of hadron interaction information from experimental measurements.

Method: A modified Vincent--Phatak method is employed within the Lippmann--Schwinger equation framework for accurate treatment of short-distance Coulomb interactions while maintaining a unified approach to strong interactions.

Result: The formalism accurately predicts interactions in the $S=-4$ pseudoscalar-baryon decuplet system, including specific charged channels, and has been validated against the well-established $pp$ system. Corrections for short-distance interaction potential are quantified.

Conclusion: A phenomenological parameter is introduced to handle contributions from both finite source size and off-shell interaction structure, aiding in the model-independent extraction of hadron-hadron interactions from femtoscopic data.

Abstract: We present a momentum-space framework for the consistent treatment of Coulomb interactions in femtoscopic correlation functions based on a modified Vincent--Phatak method that is more amenable to numerical implementation. The formalism provides a practical approach to incorporating Coulomb effects at the short distances relevant for femtoscopy within the Lippmann--Schwinger equation, while preserving a unified treatment of the strong interaction. As an application, we study the $S=-4$ pseudoscalar--baryon decuplet interaction in the $\bar{K}Ω$ system and present predictions for the singly and doubly negatively charged channels, $\bar{K}^0Ω^-$ and $K^-Ω^-$. As an additional validation of the formalism, we have also applied it to the well-studied $pp$ system. We further assess the limitations of the asymptotic wave-function approximation and quantify corrections accounting for the short-distance structure of the interaction potential. We introduce a phenomenological parameter that effectively absorbs contributions from both the finite source size and the off-shell structure of the interaction, the latter being one of the main obstacles to extracting detailed information on hadron--hadron interactions from femtoscopic measurements in a model-independent way.

</details>


### [170] [TRSMScans](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11571)
*Tania Robens,Roxana Rodriguez,Manuel Samaniego,Jason Veatch*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new tool for calculating cross section predictions for physics scenarios with additional scalars, particularly for asymmetric production processes, and demonstrates its application on the Two-Real-Singlet Model while discussing its code structure and usage.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To facilitate the prediction of maximal cross sections in new physics scenarios involving additional scalar states, enhancing tools available for particle physics research.

Method: A new scan tool is developed, based on the ScannerS framework, focusing on asymmetric processes like p p -> h3 -> h1 h2, and designed for extensibility to other production and decay modes.

Result: The tool's first application on the Two-Real-Singlet Model compares favorably with LHC collaboration results, demonstrating its effectiveness in predicting cross sections.

Conclusion: The paper provides insights into the tool's structure and methodology, emphasizing its potential for broader application in particle physics beyond the initial scope.

Abstract: In this work, we propose a new scan tool that automatically calculates maximal cross section predictions for a new physics scenario with additional scalar states. While the tool is currently optimized for asymmetric production and decay processes in the form of p p -> h3 -> h1 h2, where hi denote CP even neutral scalars, in principle it can be extended to include any production and decay mode. As the code builds largely on the structure of the publicly available code ScannerS, it can in principle be extended to any other model contained within this tool. We here show first applications for a specific model, the Two-Real-Singlet Model (TRSM), and compare to publicly available results from the LHC collaborations from the previous runs. We also in detail discuss the structure of the code, including code installation, usage, as well as underlying algorithms.

</details>


### [171] [Thermal Dilepton Polarization under Rotation or Magnetic Field in Heavy-ion Collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11634)
*Haowen He,Bowen Hou,Xingyang Wang,Minghua Wei*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates thermal dilepton production in quark-gluon plasma, focusing on virtual photon polarization influenced by vorticity and magnetic fields, showing the unique effects these have on dilepton signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the effects of vorticity and magnetic fields on virtual photon polarization and its implications in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

Method: The study uses a modified quark propagator in an external field to derive the electromagnetic spectral function and the spin density matrix elements of virtual photons, evaluating anisotropy coefficients in the produced dileptons.

Result: Distinct invariant mass spectra of dilepton polarization are observed, differing based on the influences of vorticity and magnetic fields, illustrating their unique contributions.

Conclusion: Dilepton polarization can serve as a sensitive probe for vorticity and magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, enhancing the understanding of quark-gluon plasma properties.

Abstract: Dilepton (Virtual photon) polarization is characterized by anisotropic coefficients $λ_θ$, $λ_φ$, and $λ_{θφ}$, which are expected to be influenced by vorticity and magnetic fields. This work investigates thermal dilepton production in a quark-gluon plasma via the quark-antiquark annihilation process $q\bar{q} \to γ^* \to l^+l^-$. Virtual photon polarization can be induced by both the spin polarization of quarks and the anisotropy of their momentum distribution in the medium. By employing the modified quark propagator under an external field, we derive the electromagnetic spectral function in a hot medium. Based on the spin-projection decomposition of the spectral function, the spin density matrix elements of the virtual photon and the anisotropy coefficients for the emitted dileptons are determined. Due to the distinct effects of vorticity and magnetic fields on the quark propagator, the resulting invariant mass spectra of dilepton polarization exhibit characteristic differences. Furthermore, our study reveals the response of dilepton polarization signals to external fields of varying strengths, suggesting dilepton polarization as a complementary and sensitive probe for both vorticity and magnetic fields in relativistic heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [172] [Antenna subtraction at NNLO for heavy quark pair production at hadron colliders: the non-diagonal channels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11741)
*Aude Gehrmann-De Ridder,João Pires*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a detailed calculation of heavy-quark pair production at hadron colliders through the next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) QCD using the antenna subtraction method, focusing on $qq$, $qq'$, and $qg$ channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to enhance the understanding of heavy-quark pair production processes at hadron colliders by providing a comprehensive NNLO treatment, which is essential for precision predictions in particle physics.

Method: The authors employ the antenna subtraction formalism to derive integrated subtraction contributions for the $qq$, $qq'$ and $qg$ partonic channels, extending the integrated antenna library to include processes with massive fermions.

Result: The paper completes the NNLO calculation for the mentioned channels, introducing new elements such as massive soft factors and validating the results through infrared pole cancellation and numerical comparisons.

Conclusion: The findings represent a significant advancement toward a fully differential NNLO approach for heavy-quark pair production, improving the theoretical framework within the antenna subtraction formalism.

Abstract: We present the calculation involving the $qq$, $qq'$ and $qg$ partonic channels contributing to heavy-quark pair production at hadron colliders through next-to-next-to-leading order in QCD using the antenna subtraction formalism. The calculation is performed in full colour and implemented within the NNLOJET framework. Building upon previously available results, we complete the NNLO treatment of the $qq$ and $qq'$ channels by deriving the integrated subtraction contributions required at the real virtual and double-virtual levels. We further present for the first time the NNLO antenna subtraction terms related to the $qg$-channel in full colour. The calculation requires several new ingredients, including massive soft factors and convolutions involving integrated massive antennae thereby extending the integrated antenna library to cope with processes with massive fermions. We validate the calculation through analytic infrared pole cancellation and numerical comparisons with existing results. These results complete the NNLO description of the $qq$, $qq'$, and $qg$ channels and constitute an important step towards a fully differential NNLO treatment of heavy-quark pair production within the antenna subtraction formalism.

</details>


### [173] [A Standard Model analysis of $D^0 \to π^- π^+, K^- K^+, K_{\rm S}^0 K_{\rm S}^0$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11845)
*Robert Fleischer,Maria Laura Piscopo,K. Keri Vos,B. Yağmur Zubaroğlu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes singly Cabibbo-suppressed charm decays through the Standard Model, finding significant non-factorizable effects and predicting low direct CP asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the Standard Model's accuracy in describing specific charm decays and motivate future precision measurements.

Method: Utilizing factorization and isospin symmetry to constrain non-factorizable effects and $U$-spin breaking, while analyzing measured branching fractions.

Result: Corrections of about 50% are necessary to explain the decay modes, and the derived direct CP asymmetry for $D^0\to K_{\rm S}^0K_{\rm S}^0$ is at most at the per-mille level.

Conclusion: The findings highlight both the challenges and the avenues for advancements in measuring charm decay processes.

Abstract: We investigate the Standard Model (SM) description of the singly Cabibbo-suppressed charm decays $D^0\to π^-π^+$, $D^0\to K^-K^+$ and $D^0\to K_{\rm S}^0K_{\rm S}^0$. Using factorisation together with isospin symmetry, we constrain non-factorisable effects and the associated $U$-spin breaking required by the measured branching fractions. We find that corrections of order $50\%$ are sufficient to describe all modes, which although sizeable, are not unexpected for hadronic charm decays. Using the constraints from the branching fractions, we derive SM predictions for the direct CP asymmetry in $D^0\to K_{\rm S}^0K_{\rm S}^0$, finding it to be at most at the per-mille level in our benchmark scenario, providing motivation for future precision measurements.

</details>


<div id='hep-ex'></div>

# hep-ex [[Back]](#toc)

### [174] [Observation of new excited $\itΣ_{c}^{0}$ states in the $B^-\rightarrow \itΛ^+_c\overline{p}π^{-}$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10595)

Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the decay of $B^-$ into $reve{Λ}^+_car{p}π^{-}$, discovering new baryon states and detailing their properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study aims to enhance the understanding of charm baryon spectroscopy and dynamics through detailed decay analysis.

Method: Amplitude analysis of the $B^-ightarrow reve{Λ}^+_car{p}π^{-}$ decay using proton-proton collision data from the LHCb detector.

Result: Identification of known baryons and the discovery of new states, $reve{Σ}_c(2900)^0$ and $reve{Σ}_c(3200)^0$, with their masses and widths determined via Breit–Wigner modeling.

Conclusion: Findings expand charm baryon spectroscopy and yield important insights into charm baryon structure.

Abstract: An amplitude analysis of the $B^-\rightarrow \itΛ^+_c\overline{p}π^{-}$ decay is performed using proton-proton collision data, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $9\text{ fb}^{-1}$, collected with the \mbox{LHCb} detector at center-of-mass energies of 7, 8, and 13$\text{ TeV}$. In the $\itΛ^+_cπ^{-}$ invariant-mass spectrum, the known $\itΣ_c(2455)^0$, $\itΣ_c(2520)^0$, $\itΣ_c(2800)^0$ baryons are observed, along with two new states: $\itΣ_c(2900)^0$ and $\itΣ_c(3200)^0$. The masses and widths of the three high-mass states, modeled with Breit--Wigner line shapes, are determined. This result expands the landscape of charm baryon spectroscopy and provides crucial data for understanding charm baryon structure and dynamics.

</details>

Varghese,I. Vasilyev,G. Vasseur,E. Villa,U. Virginet,T. Vladisavljevic,T. Wachala,H. T. Wallace,J. G. Walsh,D. Wark,M. O. Wascko,A. Weber,R. Wendell,M. J. Wilking,C. Wilkinson,C. Winterstein,C. Wret,J. Xia,Z. Xie,K. Yamamoto,T. Yamamoto,T. Yamazumi,C. Yanagisawa,Y. Yang,T. Yano,N. Yershov,U. Yevarouskaya,M. Yokoyama,Y. Yoshimoto,N. Yoshimura,A. Zalewska,J. Zalipska,G. Zarnecki,J. Zhang,X. Y. Zhao,H. Zheng,H. Zhong,M. Ziembicki,M. Zito*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of muon-neutrino cross section on oxygen with specific final states using T2K ND280 detector reveals deficiencies in current neutrino models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide insights into nuclear effects and improve systematic uncertainties in neutrino oscillation experiments.

Method: Simultaneous extraction of cross section for carbon and oxygen targets using double-differential measurements based on kinematic imbalance observables.

Result: None of the tested neutrino event generators accurately describe data across all measured phase space regions.

Conclusion: The findings indicate necessary improvements in neutrino-nucleus interaction modeling for future experiments.

Abstract: We report the first measurement of muon-neutrino charged-current cross section as a function of kinematic imbalance (KI) observables on oxygen with no pions and at least one proton in the final state, using the T2K ND280 detector. The cross section is extracted simultaneously for carbon and oxygen targets and double-differentially as a function of several KI observables, providing new insight into the modeling of nuclear effects. This joint measurement offers direct sensitivity to the correlations between two targets, a key ingredient for reducing systematic uncertainties in neutrino oscillation experiments that employ multiple target nuclei, such as T2K and Hyper-Kamiokande. Comparisons with predictions from widely used neutrino event generators show that none of the models fully describe the data across all regions of measured phase space. These results highlight possible directions where improvements in neutrino-nucleus interaction modeling are needed for current and future neutrino oscillation experiments.

</details>

Y. Zhao,H. Zheng,H. Zhong,M. Ziembicki,M. Zito*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First joint measurement of muon neutrino CC$0πNp$ interactions shows discrepancies with existing models, emphasizing the need for refined nuclear modeling in neutrino studies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To evaluate muon neutrino interactions on carbon and oxygen targets, enhancing understanding of neutrino oscillations.

Method: Utilized ND280 detector of T2K experiment, focusing on kinematic imbalance observables and systematic uncertainty evaluations for signal extraction.

Result: Findings reveal inadequacies in current neutrino-nucleus interaction models, particularly in relation to kinematic imbalance observables.

Conclusion: The study underscores the necessity for improved nuclear modeling in neutrino interaction generators for better precision in oscillation measurements.

Abstract: We present the first joint measurement of muon neutrino CC$0πNp$ interactions on carbon and oxygen targets, in two double-differential kinematic imbalance (KI) observable spaces, $δp_{T}$-$δα_{T}$ and $p_{N}$-$\cosθ_μ$. The measurement employs the ND280 detector of the T2K experiment and includes a detailed description of the event selection used to define signal and control regions, the evaluation of systematic uncertainties, and the signal extraction procedure, together with validation studies supporting a robust cross-section measurement. The results of this analysis indicate that current neutrino-nucleus interaction models do not adequately describe the data, and demonstrate the strong discriminating power of KI observables. This measurement highlights the need for improved theoretical nuclear modeling within neutrino interaction generators to achieve increased precision in neutrino oscillation measurements.

</details>


### [177] [Search for dark matter in a signature with a four-prong large-radius jet in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11016)
*CMS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This study presents a search for nonprompt dark matter candidates associated with an initial-state radiation jet, utilizing a CMS detector at the LHC. It finds no significant excess over the standard model and sets upper limits on signal strength based on mediator mass and coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a novel search for nonprompt dark matter candidates that interact with standard model particles through a heavy mediator, enhancing understanding of dark matter properties and interactions.

Method: Analysis of proton-proton collision data collected from 2016 to 2018 at 13 TeV, employing a jet substructure tagger based on a graph neural network to identify large-radius jets indicative of dark matter signatures.

Result: No significant excess over the standard model expectation was observed, with estimated background contributions derived from data analysis.

Conclusion: Upper limits at 95% confidence level on signal strength are established as functions of mediator mass or coupling, marking the first search of its kind in this specific topology.

Abstract: A search for a pair of nonprompt dark matter (DM) candidates produced in association with an initial-state radiation jet, in a signature containing a four-prong large-radius jet, is presented. The signal model contains a heavy vector or axial-vector mediator, which produces long-lived dark-sector particles that decay to a stable DM particle and a light boson, which decays to quarks. The analysis is based on data collected in the years 2016$-$2018 with the CMS detector at the LHC in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}$ = 13 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 138 fb$^{-1}$. Signal candidates feature large-radius jets, which are identified using a jet substructure tagger based on a graph neural network. The large-radius jet aims to reconstruct the decay of light DM mediators into four quarks, which are produced in association with two stable DM particles. The standard model background contributions are estimated from data using dedicated control regions. The missing transverse momentum spectrum is probed for a potential signal over the expected background. No significant excess over the standard model expectation is observed. Upper limits at 95% confidence level are set on the signal strength as functions of either the mediator mass or the relevant coupling. This is the first search for a pair of nonprompt DM candidates in the Lorentz-boosted topology, characterized by a large-radius jet and large missing transverse momentum.

</details>


### [178] [Antineutron reconstruction in electromagnetic calorimeters with mixed-representation learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11139)
*Yangu Li,Hongtian Yu,Yuyang Huang,Zhi Cao,Yunxuan Song,Yunfan Liu,Yajun Mao,YangHeng Zheng,Xiao-Rui Lyu,Qixiang Ye*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents a Mixed-representation Calorimetric Network (MrCAL) to accurately reconstruct antineutrons in electromagnetic calorimeters by analyzing real collision data, significantly improving momentum direction and magnitude measurements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Addressing the challenges in reconstructing long-lived neutral hadrons within conventional electromagnetic calorimeters, which struggle with hadron-nucleus interactions.

Method: The paper introduces a Mixed-representation Calorimetric Network (MrCAL) that combines visual and sequential representation branches for object detection, improving particle identity and momentum predictions.

Result: The method increases antineutron momentum-direction reconstruction precision by up to 96% and achieves a momentum resolution of around 17% at 1 GeV/c, enabling direct momentum magnitude measurements solely from ECAL data.

Conclusion: This work enhances the capabilities of existing ECAL systems, expanding their utility for detecting final-state neutral hadrons in high-energy physics experiments.

Abstract: A long-standing bottleneck in GeV-scale accelerator experiments lies in reconstructing long-lived neutral hadrons in conventional electromagnetic calorimeters (ECALs), where hadron--nucleus interactions fall outside the detector's native response regime. In this paper, we develop a physics-inspired representation approach for antineutron reconstruction using a large corpus of real collision data. Motivated by two distinct energy deposition patterns from the penetrating high energy antineutrons in ECALs, we propose a Mixed-representation Calorimetric Network (MrCAL) that integrates complementary visual and sequential representation branches within a unified object-detection architecture. This architecture jointly predicts particle identity, momentum direction, and momentum magnitude. Our approach improves the precision of antineutron momentum-direction reconstruction by up to 96% and, for the first time, enables direct measurement of momentum magnitude solely from ECAL readouts, achieving a momentum resolution of approximately 17% at 1 GeV/c. The model maintains robust performance through comprehensive generalization tests spanning a wide variety of physics processes and background environments. This work unlocks a novel measurement capability for legacy ECAL systems at large experimental facilities, broadening their scientific scope via innovative final-state neutral-hadron detection.

</details>

Zhu,Lin Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First observation of the decay process $η_c	o p\bar{p}η$ with a significance exceeding $10σ$ using data from $ψ(3686)$ events.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the branching fraction and underlying processes of the decay $η_c 	o p\bar{p}η$ through the $ψ(3686)$ resonance.

Method: Analysis of $(2712.4 \\pm 14.3) \times 10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events at the BESIII detector, considering both resonant and non-resonant interference effects.

Result: Branching fractions measured at $(3.2 \\pm 0.1 \\pm 0.9) \times 10^{-6}$ (constructive) and $(8.7 \\pm 0.3 \\pm 2.1) \times 10^{-6}$ (destructive) for $ψ(3686)\to γη_c$, revealing highly significant interference effects.

Conclusion: Determined branching fractions for $η_c\to p\bar{p}η$ are $(0.90 \\pm 0.04 \\pm 0.21 \\pm 0.13) \times 10^{-3}$ (constructive) and $(2.42 \\pm 0.07 \\pm 0.48 \\pm 0.34) \times 10^{-3}$ (destructive), highlighting the influence of interference from $ψ(3686)$ decay.

Abstract: The decay $η_c\to p\bar{p}η$ is observed for the first time with a significance of exceeding $10σ$. It is found by analyzing $(2712.4 \pm 14.3)\times10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events accumulated at the BESIII detector. The measured branching fraction of $η_c\to p\bar{p}η$ via $ψ(3686) \to γp \bar{p} η$ is significantly influenced by the interference between the resonant $η_c$ decay and the non-resonant process $ψ(3686) \to γp \bar{p} η$ and is measured in both constructive- and destructive-interference scenarios. The joint branching fraction of $ψ(3686)\to γη_c$, $η_c\to p\bar{p}η$ is measured to be $(3.2 \pm 0.1 \pm 0.9)\times10^{-6}$ or $(8.7 \pm 0.3 \pm 2.1)\times10^{-6}$ for constructive- or destructive-interference solutions, respectively, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic. The branching fraction of $η_c\to p\bar{p}η$ is determined to be $\mathcal{B}(η_c\to p\bar{p}η)=(0.90 \pm 0.04 \pm 0.21 \pm 0.13)\times10^{-3}$ or $(2.42 \pm 0.07 \pm 0.48 \pm 0.34)\times10^{-3}$ for the two solutions, respectively, where the third uncertainties are due to the uncertainty in the branching fraction of $ψ(3686)\to γη_c$.

</details>


### [180] [Study of the proccess $e^+e^-\to π^+π^-π^0π^0η$ in the c.m. energy range 1.6--2.0 GeV with the CMD-3 detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11556)
*R. R. Akhmetshin,A. N. Amirkhanov,A. V. Anisenkova,V. M. Aulchenkoa,N. S. Bashtovoy,E. V. Bedarev,D. E. Berkaev,A. E. Bondar,A. V. Bragin,V. S. Denisov,D. A. Epifanov,L. B. Epshteyn,A. L. Erofeev,G. V. Fedotovich,A. O. Gorkovenko,A. A. Grebenuk,S. S. Gribanov,D. N. Grigoriev,F. V. Ignatov,V. L. Ivanov,A. S. Kasaev,S. V. Karpov,V. F. Kazanin,I. A. Koop,A. A. Korobov,A. N. Kozyrev,P. P. Krokovny,A. S. Kuzmin,I. B. Logashenko,P. A. Lukin,K. Yu. Mikhailov,I. V. Obraztsov,Yu. N. Pestov,E. A. Perevedentsev,V. G. Petrochenko,N. A. Petrov,A. S. Popov,Yu. A. Rogovsky,A. A. Ruban,A. E. Ryzhenenkov,A. V. Semenov,A. I. Senchenko,V. E. Shebalin,D. N. Shemyakin,B. A. Shwartz,D. B. Shwartz,E. P. Solodov,M. V. Timoshenko,V. M. Titov,A. A. Talyshev,S. S. Tolmachev,A. I. Vorobiov,I. M. Zemlyansky,D. S. Zhadan,Yu. V. Yudin*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Cross section for the process $e^+e^- \to π^+π^-π^0π^0η$ has been measured, showing contributions from several resonances.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the dynamics of the process $e^+e^- \to π^+π^-π^0π^0η$ and identify the contributing resonances.

Method: Data collected from CMD-3 detector at VEPP-2000 collider with an integrated luminosity of 372 pb$^{-1}$, resulting in 6300 signal events in the 1.6--2.0 GeV energy range.

Result: Total systematic uncertainty in the cross section is about 10"; the dynamics mainly involve the $ω(782)π^0η$ with some $a_0(980)$ resonance and $ρ(770)^{	ext{±}}$ presence confirmed in invariant mass distribution.

Conclusion: Findings enhance understanding of the resonant structure in $e^+e^- \to π^+π^-π^0π^0η$, with key resonances identified.

Abstract: The cross section of the process $e^+e^- \to π^+π^-π^0π^0η$ has been measured using a data sample with the integrated luminosity of 372 pb$^{-1}$ collected with the CMD-3 detector at the VEPP-2000 $e^+e^-$ collider. 6300$\pm$145 signal events have been selected in the center-of-mass energy range 1.6--2.0 GeV. The total systematic uncertainty of the cross section is about 10\%. The production dynamics is dominated by the $ω(782)π^0η$ final state with a possible small contribution of the $a_0(980)$ resonance in the $π^0η$ combination. We also observe a presence of the $ρ(770)^{\pm}$ resonance signal in the $π^{\pm}π^0$ invariant mass distribution.

</details>


### [181] [Machine Learning-Based Reconstruction for Resistive Silicon Sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11585)
*Alexander Aoki,Gaetano Barone,Leena Diehl,Gabriele Giacomini,Vagelis Gkougkousis,Hanshal Goyal,Rohan Kher,Daniel Li,Anna Macchiolo,Yevhenii Padnuik,Daria Senina,Samantha Sunnarborg,Jessica Tang,Alessandro Tricoli,Lixing Wang,Don C. Wong*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Explores machine-learning techniques for enhancing spatial resolution and reducing bandwidth in resistive silicon sensors for LGAD and AC-LGAD technologies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve precision timing and tracking in LGADs and AC-LGADs while addressing challenges in readout due to charge spreading and electronic noise.

Method: Utilizes machine-learning models, particularly LSTM and transformer architectures, for full-waveform reconstruction and bandwidth reduction in resistive silicon sensors.

Result: Achieves improved spatial resolution and reduced computational challenges by regularizing reconstruction using full-waveform information and applying advanced neural network models.

Conclusion: The findings suggest effective strategies for enhancing resistive silicon sensor designs while maintaining high position resolution.

Abstract: Low-Gain Avalanche Diodes (LGADs) and AC-coupled Low-Gain Avalanche Diodes (AC-LGADs) are promising technologies for precision timing and four-dimensional tracking. In AC-LGADs, the AC pad is coupled to the resistive n$^{+}$ layer through a dielectric layer, while the gain layer remains unsegmented. This structure provides a 100\% fill factor and enables good spatial resolution with a relaxed readout pitch. The same signal-sharing mechanism that makes interpolation possible complicates the readout: charge spreads across multiple pads, the useful information can approach the electronic-noise threshold, and matrix-inversion approaches can become computationally challenging and sensitive to off-diagonal noise. In this work, we study machine-learning-based reconstruction and compression for resistive silicon sensors. We use full-waveform information from correlated pads to regularise the reconstruction and extract spatial information beyond what is available from binary readouts or reduced-amplitude summaries. We first introduce recurrent neural network models based on LSTM layers, which provide a proof-of-concept implementation for full-waveform reconstruction and have been tested for FPGA deployment using \hls. We also study routes towards bandwidth reduction with waveform rasterisation and window-selection methods, and extend the approach beyond the first model to topology-agnostic transformer-based architectures that use pad coordinates as part of the input. These models are designed to support arbitrary pad counts and geometries, mitigate edge distortions, preserve approximately $10~μ\mathrm{m}$ position resolution for $500~μ\mathrm{m}\times500~μ\mathrm{m}$ pitched sensors, and guide future resistive-silicon sensor designs

</details>


### [182] [Analytical Markov Chain for Spatiotemporal Flux Evolution of the Inner Filter Effect in Fluorescent Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11815)
*Xuhui Yang,Guofu Cao*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: An analytical Markovian transport model reduces computation time in evaluating emission spectra by converting complex integrals into a simpler transition matrix, enabling fast parameter optimization despite the secondary inner filter effect distortion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The need to identify material properties and optimize detectors in multi-component fluorescent media, hindered by the distortions caused by the secondary inner filter effect.

Method: An analytical Markovian transport model utilizing Laplace transforms to convert complex convolution integrals into a discrete Markov transition matrix, reducing computational complexity.

Result: The model provides algebraic solutions for transient decay time spectra and predicts steady-state distortions, matching Monte Carlo simulation results in lineshape across various spectrometer configurations.

Conclusion: This model accelerates parameter space screening and offers key insights for detector design and event reconstruction algorithms.

Abstract: Characterizing emission and decay time spectra in multi-component fluorescent media is essential for identifying intrinsic material properties and optimizing detectors. However, wavelength evolution from the secondary inner filter effect (IFE) distorts these observable spectra. While Monte Carlo (MC) ray-tracing can simulate this distortion, accumulating adequate tracking statistics requires long computation times, which hinders parameter optimization within high-dimensional spaces. This paper presents an analytical Markovian transport model based on spatiotemporal decoupling. A Laplace transform converts the multi-nested convolution integrals over continuous domains into a discrete Markov transition matrix, reducing the computational complexity from an exponential scale with respect to wavelength bins $N_λ$ and cascade order $n$, $\mathcal{O}(N_λ^n)$, to a linear scale, $\mathcal{O}(N_λ + n)$. The resulting algebraic solutions evaluate transient decay time spectra as a continuum superposition of Gamma wave packets and predict steady-state wavelength spectrum distortions driven by the IFE within a sub-second timescale. Validations across orthogonal and front-face spectrometer configurations show that the calculated spectra match MC simulations in lineshape. This model can serve as a fast forward engine to accelerate parameter space screening, provide early-stage detector design references, and act as a physics-constrained input for event vertex reconstruction algorithms.

</details>

# physics.ins-det [[Back]](#toc)

### [183] [Quality control and quality assurance evaluation of ALFE2, a large-dynamic-range front-end ASIC de-veloped for the ATLAS Liquid Argon Calorimeter high-luminosity LHC upgrade](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10458)
*E. Buschmann,G. Carini,G. Chatzianastasiou,H. Chen,Y. Chen,M. Dabrowski,G. Deptuch,L. Duflot,M. Feo,J. Kierstead,T. Liu,H. Ma,D. Matakias,N. Morange,M. Oliveira,S. Rescia,E. Rossi,S. Tang,M. Tamari,H. Xu*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: ALFE2, a front-end ASIC for ATLAS LAr Calorimeter, shows over 85% yield in quality control tests, with no significant performance degradation post-irradiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance the performance and reliability of the ATLAS LAr Calorimeter during high-luminosity operations at the LHC.

Method: Developed a robotic system for automatic quality control, evaluated over 10% of 80,000 chips, established grading criteria, and performed irradiation tests.

Result: Achieved over 85% yield in QC tests; no significant performance degradation post-TID testing; estimated error rate of <4.6 SEUs/day for the entire ATLAS LAr Calorimeter.

Conclusion: ALFE2 meets the reliability standards necessary for its operation in the HL-LHC phase.

Abstract: ALFE2 is a front-end ASIC developed for the ATLAS Liquid Argon (LAr) Calorimeter upgrade during the High-Luminosity Large Hadron Collider (HL-LHC) phase. ALFE2 comprises four preamplifier/shaper channels, each providing two distinct gain outputs to cover a 16-bit dynamic range. A robotic system has been developed for the automatic quality control test of ALFE2, and over 10% of the 80,000 chips have been evaluated by September 2025. The evaluation has allowed us to establish grading criteria. Using these criteria, a yield of over 85% was achieved in the evaluation tests, and these criteria are now being applied to the ongoing full-production QC. Irradiation tests were also performed for the quality assurance of ALFE2. No significant performance degradation was observed during the total-ionizing-dose (TID) test. Based on the single-event effect (SEE) test results, an error rate of fewer than 4.6 single-event upsets (SEUs) per day is extrapolated for the entire ATLAS LAr Calorimeter during HL-LHC operation.

</details>


### [184] [Small but Tubby: A Magnetic Loop Antenna Made from 100 mm Copper Tubing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10828)
*Peter Märki,Markus Niese*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: This paper details the design and performance of a novel small transmitting magnetic loop antenna, focusing on its construction from large copper tubing and innovative features to minimize losses and enhance efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop an efficient small transmitting magnetic loop antenna suitable for indoor environments, with automatic adjustments for frequency, impedance matching, and azimuth.

Method: The antenna utilizes 100 mm diameter copper tubing, designed for low resistive losses. It incorporates servo motors for automatic adjustments and routes control wiring through the conductor to avoid insulation issues.

Result: The antenna achieves a wide operational frequency range (1.8 MHz to 31 MHz) with minimal losses primarily attributed to environmental factors, confirming good performance with measured H-field aligning with theoretical predictions.

Conclusion: The developed antenna is effective for indoor use, demonstrating that dissipated power mainly travels into the surrounding environment rather than the antenna itself.

Abstract: This paper presents the electrical model, key equations, and practical construction of a small transmitting magnetic loop antenna built from unusually large 100 mm diameter copper tubing. The large conductor surface area and wide-area transitions to the vacuum capacitors were designed to minimize resistive losses. The frequency range from 1.8 MHz to 31 MHz is unusually wide. Frequency, impedance matching, and azimuth are all adjusted automatically by servo motors. A novel feature is the routing of the control wiring inside the loop conductor, allowing the motor to be mounted without electrical insulation from the loop conductor. Indoor losses originate predominantly from near-field coupling to the environment rather than from the antenna itself. Temperature-rise measurements confirm that the bulk of the dissipated power is absorbed by the environment, not by the antenna components. The conducted H-field measurements demonstrate good agreement between the measured H-field and the theoretical free-space H-field calculated from the antenna geometry and an estimated loop current. The loop current was estimated from the measured antenna bandwidth and the applied transmit power. The antenna was developed for indoor operation where outdoor installation is not possible.

</details>


### [185] [Effects of irradiation by protons, neutrons, and gamma particles on electrical properties of 4H-SiC diodes and LGAD sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11663)
*Jiří Kroll,Pavla Federičová,Jan Chochol,Adam Klimsza,Jana Kozáková,Adam Kozelský,Vojtěch Kráčmar,Jiří Kvasnička,Roman Malousek,Mária Marčišovská,Michal Marčišovský,Marcela Mikeštíková,David Novák,Radek Novotný,Peter Slovák,Radim Špetík,Peter Švihra,Pavel Tůma*

Main category: physics.ins-det

TL;DR: This study examines the impact of irradiation on the electrical properties of 4H-SiC PN diodes and LGADs, revealing significant changes in leakage current and capacitance due to radiation-induced defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the suitability of 4H-SiC as a radiation-hard detector by assessing the modifications in electrical characteristics under different types of radiation exposure.

Method: Conducting IV and CV measurements on 4H-SiC devices exposed to various radiation sources including protons, neutrons, and gamma rays, assessing effects on leakage current and capacitance.

Result: Irradiation led to changes in leakage current, capacitance, and effective doping, indicating significant radiation-induced compensation and depletion region expansion, especially under high fluences.

Conclusion: The findings demonstrate that radiation alters effective space charge and electric-field distribution in 4H-SiC devices, highlighting the material's sensitivity to both ionizing and hadronic radiation.

Abstract: 4H-SiC is a wide-bandgap semiconductor with high displacement threshold energy, large critical electric field, and low intrinsic carrier concentration, making it attractive for radiation-hard detector applications. In this work, we investigate the electrical characteristics of 4H-SiC P$^{+}$-in-N (PN) diodes and Low-Gain Avalanche Detectors (LGADs) fabricated by onsemi before and after irradiation by 24 GeV/c protons, reactor neutrons, and $^{60}$Co gamma rays. Current-voltage (IV) and capacitance-voltage (CV) measurements were performed at room temperature for proton fluences up to $1\times10^{16}\;\mathrm{protons/cm^2}$, neutron fluences up to $1\times10^{18}\;\mathrm{1\;MeV\;n_{eq}/cm^2}$, and total ionizing doses up to 300 kGy.
  Hadron irradiation induces pronounced changes in both leakage current and bulk capacitance, consistent with radiation-induced formation of deep acceptor-like defects and strong compensation of the originally N-type material. For high proton fluences, the leakage current decreases and the bulk capacitance becomes bias-independent, indicating effective compensation of the epitaxial layer. Extreme neutron fluences lead to a substantial expansion of the depleted region into the originally highly doped substrate, as inferred from the measured capacitance values. Gamma irradiation up to 300 kGy results in significantly modified capacitance behavior, suggesting reduction of the effective doping concentration in the epitaxial and multiplication layers.
  The results demonstrate that radiation-induced compensation strongly modifies the effective space charge in 4H-SiC devices at high hadron fluences, while the leakage current is influenced by the enlarged depletion volume together with field-enhanced and surface-related generation mechanisms. In contrast, ionizing damage primarily affects the effective doping and electric-field distribution.

</details>
